ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u15	I'm sorry. Dear Members and honourable Members, [[Voice from the room: Wait a minute, put the mask on.]] The overarching objective of the proposed amendment of the Law of 9 November 2017 on the Institute of Solidarity and Fortitude of Witold Pilecki, hereinafter referred to as the Act, is to create the possibility of creating subsidiaries of the Institute of Solidarity and Fortitude of Witold Pilecki, hereinafter referred to as the Institute. The Institute was created for interdisciplinary and international reflection on key issues of the 20th century: two totalitarianisms, German and Soviet, as well as the consequences of their actions. The implementation of the Institute's mission in this area requires action at national level, but more importantly, at international level. I remind all Members that in 2018 we created a branch of the institute in Berlin, which is very active, very visible, also in the German media, which has extremely positive reviews of the activities undertaken, exhibitions and teacher training. This time we would also like to create institutes outside the European Union, especially in New York. This amendment is needed precisely to enable such an institute to function in New York, that is, in the United States of America, where Poland, I would say, has the weakest potential of historical policy, educational potential. That's why there's an amendment. So in addition to very small things, such as using the shortened name of the Pilecki Institute instead of the full name of the Institute of Solidarity and Fortitude, we also wanted to clarify the rules in such a way that the Institute could operate outside the Union. First of all, the proposed amendment will enable the Institute to carry out its statutory tasks by producing and co-production documentary and feature films. These are the minor changes. I would also like to emphasise that the whole of the proposed solutions is similar to the one provided for in Article. 7c paragraph. 1 Act of 20 December 1996 on the Polish Institute of International Affairs. Exactly on the same legal skeleton a branch of PISM was established in the United States, among others. was also a model for the Pilecki Institute. I think all these changes are clear. I would very much like to answer the questions raised by Members of the European Parliament in connection with this revision. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u17	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! This amendment should probably be adopted by the Sejm without great political disputes, because it serves all of us. This is to extend the capacity of the Pilecki Institute also outside the European Union. At the moment we have such regulations, which cause that it can only be an institution, which is a subsidiary of the existing Pilecki Institute in Warsaw, which is a limitation to activity only within the European Union. The Institute in Berlin - which has been working very intensively through 2019 and a little less intensively in terms of such contacts, due to the pandemic, in 2020 - proves how much such an institution is needed also outside Poland, an institution that disseminates knowledge about the heroism of Poles and the martyrdom of Poles struggling with barbarians from the West and the East. It is also necessary for digitization of archives, which can be found and maintained and made available to Polish scientists in cooperation with the German site. It is also necessary to disseminate knowledge about heroes who rescued Polish citizens, Poland and the Second Republic against the dangers posed by two totalitarianisms. The story of how Poles managed, how Poland managed to deal with these two totalitarians, is still well-developed by us, even for our domestic use. We don't know enough, for example. about the actions of extermination of Polish intelligence, it is not described properly, there are not yet examined archives, there are collected certificates of those who are still alive, or their direct descendants. This is of course the result of the Polish People's Republic, communism, which was not conducive to such research, and unfortunately the last 30 years, when the Third Republic was rather sponsoring the stories that they wanted to bring out these cards of Polish history, which are rather embarrassing - of course, as in every nation, there are also individuals who denied this noble and heroic attitude that we have to describe. The Institute in New York or the Institute in Israel, because I know that such plans are also plans, will be very much needed to disseminate knowledge, simply knowledge about our history of the 20th century, which is either familiarized or lied to there. But it is also necessary to face history in partnership, in conversation with historians from that side and in confrontation with documentation, with archives as ours as well as those found or found elsewhere in the world. The Pilecki Institute shows that it is worth investing in institutions that guard Polish history, which guard Polish consciousness. We really have a lot to do in this regard, and I am very pleased that, thanks also to Minister Magdalena Gawin, this institute could have been established. I hope that this amendment will enable us to broaden the scope for this very, very important institution. I'll talk about one of the latest initiatives. A committee was set up to celebrate the year of the Ładosia Group. The High Sejm established this year as the Year of the Ładosia Group. It is the Pilecki Institute in Berlin that is the organizer, it is the centre that brings together the work of this committee, which brings together the descendants of the Ładosia group, who rescued the Jews and the descendants of those who were rescued, as well as the representation of the Polish parliament. Therefore, the Law and Justice included in the way the Pilecki Institute is doing, supports this amendment in the hope that this will allow for the expansion of spectacular activities in such a short time. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u65	Dear Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the government draft law amending the Bank Guarantee Fund Act, the deposit guarantee scheme and compulsory restructuring and some other laws, print No 1164. The aim of this draft law is to implement the European Union's legislation, which is to increase the resilience of financial institutions. The financial crisis in 2007 and 2008 was a direct impetus for reform by the European Union in the field of financial services. After this crisis, the European Union has implemented important reforms in the area of financial services aimed at increasing the resilience of financial institutions. The lack of appropriate crisis management and resolution regulations has forced the governments of most countries to rescue banks from bankruptcy and the consequences of the financial crisis. The continued impact of these measures on public finances and the burden on the public of the restructuring and bankruptcy costs of banks led to the need to develop a new approach to managing banking crises. As a result, the responsibility of bank owners and creditors has been increased, not taxpayers. The relevant Directives of the European Parliament and of the Council of 2014 were implemented in Poland in the form of the Act of 10 June 2016 on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the Deposit Insurance Scheme and the Forced Restructuring. It is worth mentioning that on 16 April 2019. The European Parliament has adopted a banking package aimed at finalising reforms involving extensive changes to banking regulation. The solutions adopted will have a significant impact on bank capital requirements, risk models, financial structure and reporting systems. The government bill, which we are now discussing, introduces in the Bank Guarantee Fund Act two levels of designation of the so-called. MREL, making them dependent on the forced restructuring strategy adopted. This strategy depends on the decision of the compulsory restructuring authority. The government project introduces minimum levels of MREL level. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In a detailed discussion of this draft law at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance and the Permanent Sub-Committee on Financial Institutions, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice tabled 11 amendments at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance and 31 amendments at the level of the Sub-Committee respectively. The purpose of these amendments was to clarify the various provisions contained in the Act and some of these amendments were of a regular, linguistic and legislative nature. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend the adoption by the High House of this bill, so important for the Polish banking system and its clients. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u71	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Polish Coalition - Polish People's Party, the Union of European Democrats and Conservatives, I would like to present our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Bank Guarantee Fund Act, the deposit guarantee scheme and forced restructuring and some other laws, prints no. 1164 and 1335. As we know, the project adapts Polish law to European Union legislation. This is primarily about the system of securities preparing the banking system for possible repetitions of the crisis that took place in 2007 and 2008. This is particularly the case for the compulsory restructuring of banks, which is extremely important from the point of view of those who have entrusted their funds to these banks. The minimum requirement for own funds and eligible liabilities has been introduced, which aims to strengthen capacity, inter alia. banks to cover losses and recapitalisations. It is also about banks being able to restructure efficiently and quickly while maintaining the stability of the entire financial system as well as using minimum public resources. In this way, during and immediately after the compulsory restructuring procedure, these institutions will be able to continue to perform their most important functions without endangering the risk of financial stability or the involvement of taxpayers' funds. Two levels of designation of MREL have been introduced, making them dependent on the forced restructuring strategy adopted. Provisions have been laid down to protect retail investors from the risk of investing in bank debt securities which may be subject to debt write-off or conversion by the competent authority after compliance with the conditions defined by Union law and the relevant decision has been taken by the competent authority. The conditions for the use of government financial stabilisation instruments and the role of Minister for State Assets in this process have been clarified. In the case of the Attorney General of the Republic of Poland, among others. Interpretative doubts concerning the outsourcing of opinions by legal persons with the participation of the State Treasury or State legal persons have been resolved. These changes will certainly make banks more resistant to different kinds of shocks. The aim of the project is for banks to be able to restructure efficiently and quickly while maintaining the stability of the financial system as a whole, as well as with minimal use of public funds. I think that this will also enable these institutions to continue to perform their most important functions in the course of the compulsory restructuring procedure and immediately after its completion without risking the involvement of taxpayers' funds or financial stability. For the reasons I mentioned, I think it's good for the High Sejm to continue to proceed with this bill. I think that as a club we will also be actively involved in this work. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u97	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Starting with questions about the duration of the procedure and the date of entry into force, I would like to point out that this is a project that has been really long-standing, starting with the arrangements, the public consultations that took place two tours, with all stakeholders. Of course, these entities were invited, while there were individual invitations for the Polish Bank Association, for entities, financial institutions. These two rounds of agreement have led to the fact that, in principle, this project has actually been agreed. In the case of comments that we could not take into account, it has been explained why we do not take them into account. This is also seen in the protocol that hangs on the pages of the Government Legislative Center and has been handed over to the High House. Then, after the project was sent to the High House, it was read in plenary at the Sejm. The project was in the Committee on Public Finance, then in a permanent subcommittee within the Committee on Public Finance, and then returned to the Committee on Public Finance. We are now in the process of discussing this project in the plenary room. I think that each of us is able to quote a number of projects that have been dealt with at a much faster pace. This project was really conducted according to the art of legislation. There was as much time as you expected. The subcommittee meeting lasted, as I recall, eight hours. For eight hours we answered all the questions asked by the members of the subcommittee, there was no acceleration by President Janczyk. The same was true of the Committee on Public Finance. We answered all the questions, both orally, at the committee meeting and in writing. Therefore, it is a little difficult for me to accept the allegation that this project was being dealt with at an express rate. However, the project, which was examined by the next three meetings of the Sejm, is a well-worked project. I would also point out that the project does not have 700 pages. The project consists of 700 pages together with the justification, the OSR and all the attachments added to it. The normative content itself, if I remember correctly, is about 150 pages. I'd like to correct that, too. On the other hand, the question has also arisen as to why this implementation is taking place so late. Firstly, this is another project from a series of implementation projects in this area, this is another such directive. In the law of financial institutions, the European Union accepts, in principle, a rhetoric in which another directive replaces another. And we have numbers 3, 4, 5. Probably again under these directives we will come with implementations of the third and fourth. The deadline for implementation actually expired in December 2020 and I would point out that the European Commission has always adopted a certain implementation reserve. Having a choice of two rounds of inter-ministerial arrangements and consultations, quietly working through this at the Sejm stage and, on the other hand, chasing with a deadline for implementation, it seems to me that the more appropriate way is to work this project calmly, to talk with private stakeholders, and to talk in the Sejm rather than real rushing which you are accusing here. That is why we have these few months of delay, as the European Commission knows - we are in constant contact, here I would like to calm down - so that we can say with a clear conscience that we have done our utmost to ensure that this project is renegotiated. In response to the question on the possible effects of the implementation on the banking sector, I would like to stress that implementation in principle does not impose new burdens. It only modifies the calculation of the MREL requirement, which is also currently in force. The modification will in principle be beneficial for banks as the sector will be more stable and will not have to meet the requirements of the TLAC in parallel. So here we are equalizing and simplifying administrative issues. I cannot, of course, rule out that for individual entities this change in the method of calculation will generate some additional costs, because, of course, MREL, apart from the methods of calculation, also changes the catalogue of instruments that we include in it. On the other hand, I do not have the knowledge and I cannot have it, which for a particular entity such effects will be because they are covered by bank secrecy. And that's why we had two rounds of arrangement so that each bank, first of all, could be familiar with these new requirements, second, could prepare for them, thirdly, if the entity concerned were threatened with instability, could draw attention to it. Such a threat does not fall within the terms of the agreement. The question concerning the Podkarpackie Cooperative Bank, which does not directly concern this project, of course I will answer. The question was, if this law had already been in force, it would have helped in this situation. This would not be the case here, because the Podkarpackie Cooperative Bank experienced this situation mainly because of the irregularities found in the management, and secondly, the competent institutions in this area, namely KNF, BFG, were not able to find an entity that would be interested in taking rescue action. That is why we are implementing in national law a number of EU rules which simplify, strengthen the resolution procedure, which strengthen the emergency instruments that the State has at its disposal so that such situations do not occur. The State, in order to prevent situations like the Podkarpackie Cooperative Bank, but also with any other entity, must implement it, must amend the law, however incriminating it is to the Ministry of Finance and to the Sejm, in order to prevent such situations. And that's what's happening, which is also proof of the situation in recent months. I will answer the question of the changes concerning auditors: on the one hand, you are asking here when you will finally be stable, when you will be strong, when you will protect weaker financial market participants, consumers from unfair, professional - let us not be afraid of that word - banksters. If we look at the statutory auditor's bill and the entity concerned, that is, you, the Polish Audit Agency... Mr. President was also at the subcommittee meeting, and at the committee meeting, so he also answered these questions. And here was full transparency, transparency and openness on our part. It is yours that serves in situations where we strengthen the supervision of those who should properly audit the banking sector. If we look at what was the main cause or one of the main causes of the GetBack scandal, it will turn out that it was incorrect supervision of private sector institutions over financial sector institutions. You're not supposed to allow any more of this to happen. On the question of why these changes concerning auditors, the answer is this: that is why we introduce them, so that the Polish state has real instruments of control for those who, at the same time, can lead to situations where a number of consumers, weaker financial market participants, must be protected from those who may use some ignorance, a lack of professionalism. [[Don't bullshit me. Just tell me who's behind this. Who's behind this?]] Mr. Speaker! The amendment is tabled by the club, by the Member of Law and Justice, the government supported it and was supported by the President of the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency. I'll answer the rest of the questions in writing. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u103	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The aim of the project is to clearly indicate that all land, buildings and buildings in the area of public airport parts are exempt from real estate taxes, including land which is not occupied by buildings and buildings located in that area. The draft law concerns the amendment of the article. Paragraph 7 1 point 3 of the Act of 12 January 1991 on local taxes and charges, which currently reads: Buildings, buildings and land seized under them in the area of parts of airports of public use are exempt from property tax. The wording of this provision so far is ambiguous and can be interpreted as meaning that only buildings and buildings and land occupied by these buildings and buildings in the area of public airport parts are exempt from property tax. Therefore, it was not explicitly recorded whether or not the other land of the air part of the airport is subject to tax. This was interpreted differently by the municipalities, and the ambiguity of the situation led to the emergence of case law by the administrative courts in this area which had been broken out of the practice of law by the majority of the municipalities, resulting in ambiguity and legal uncertainty. I will only quote the opinion of the Sejm Analysis Office, of course part of it: Buildings, buildings and land seized under them are exempt from property tax in the current state of law in the area of public airport parts. Such a design of the exemption leads to interpretation disputes and, as is apparent from practice, the possibility for the tax authority to limit the scope of the statutory exemption, i.e. to cover only land built in buildings and buildings, while to tax unbuilt areas located at airports of public use. Adoption of the proposed amendment and new wording of point 1 in Article. Paragraph 7 1 will mean that the real estate tax exemption will cover all land, buildings and buildings in the area of public airport parts, including those land which are not occupied by buildings and buildings located in that area. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! The Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Committee on Public Finances, after first reading, have tabled only one amendment, it is in the report. The amendment concerns Article. 2, the date of entry into force of the Act of 1 July this year shall be postponed. as at 1 January 2022 In other words, in simplest terms, the everyday language, the public language, is about whether or not. Grass parts that are part of airports are to be taxed or not, because there are no buildings or structures. They are not supposed to be, and some municipalities have interpreted that they can be taxed. And this record clearly resolves this problem. The Law and Justice Club will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u104	Thank you very much, sir. And please, Mrs Zofia Czernow, the Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u105	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition has the honour to present its position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on local taxes and charges, prints No 1228 and 1370. The Parliamentary bill concerns the extension of the scope of the tax exemption on the property of parts of airports of public use. The current wording of the article. Paragraph 7 Point 1 (3) of the Local Tax and Tax Act indicates that buildings, buildings and land occupied by these buildings and buildings in the area of public service airports' parts are exempt from property tax. This means that, in the current legal state, other land located in the area of airport parts which are not occupied by buildings and buildings is subject to taxation. The proposed amendment means that the exemption from property tax would be subject to all land, buildings and constructions in the area of public airport parts, including those land which are not occupied by buildings and buildings located in that area. In order to clarify the substance of the doubts and the problem concerning this project, it is necessary to answer the question: What is the aviation part of the airport? According to the Aviation Law, the aerodrome's flight section is an area permanently intended for aircraft take-off and landing and associated aircraft traffic, including equipment for handling that traffic to which access is controlled. There would be no doubt in this regard if not for the fact that the air part of individual airports is determined by the President of the Civil Aviation Office and there are situations where the area of the air part of the airport is included in the facilities in which other activities are carried out. I would like to stress that this amendment does not exclude from tax exemptions buildings where their activities are not carried out in any way related to aviation activities and have a clearly commercial purpose. This is not a good solution when a commercial activity is exempt from real estate tax, as this contradicts the essence of taxes, which should be common and based on the same criteria for all. The Civic Coalition Club supports the draft tax exemption law on the area of airport parts, but at the same time introduces an amendment aimed at excluding from the exemption other business activities not related to the operation of public service airports. The Civic Coalition also supports the activity of aeroclubs - and absolutely so that there is no doubt. When considering this draft law, it is absolutely necessary to clarify the rules on property tax in railway areas. The amendments concern the same law and even the same article. 7. The amendment to the rules on the taxation of railway sites introduced in 2016, whereby land, buildings and buildings forming part of the railway infrastructure are exempt from property tax, irrespective of the purpose for which they are used, has created a huge tax gap which has a huge impact on local budgets. There are numerous examples that indicate that railway areas are used for highly commercial purposes, in no way unrelated to railway infrastructure, such as shopping galleries, mines, parking lots, etc. These facilities are completely exempt from real estate tax and are exempt from the tax of all registration parcels. It is sufficient that these are railway areas, regardless of the extent to which they are occupied under railway infrastructure. In extreme cases - on the basis of examples - 1% is used space for railway infrastructure, or even less, and the remaining 99% is used for commercial activities. The losses for local governments amount to more than several hundred million zlotys per year. In order to clarify the rules on real estate tax in the aerodrome area, it would be appropriate to clarify the rules on railway sites in order to eliminate the existing tax gap. We therefore make the second amendment, which concerns the clarification of the real estate tax exemptions for railway areas and consists of the exemption from real estate exemptions in railway areas used for other purposes. High Seym! Clear and precise tax law is our common goal. There is a chance that this submission will sort out imprecise and even unfair solutions (the Bell) in the field of local taxes. I am convinced that the High Sejm will accept these proposals. I'm making amends. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u153	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Yesterday, at the meeting of the Energy and Treasury Committee, the first reading of the draft law on the amendment of the Law on the monitoring and control of fuel quality and certain other laws, the printing of the Seym No 1346 took place. During the committee meeting, a representative of the government discussed the draft and the discussion of the members of the committee was concluded with the adoption of the draft for further consideration. Six substantive and legislative-precise amendments were tabled during the detailed discussion of the project. All amendments were accepted without objection. The project concerns the implementation of the National Reduction Objective, the NCR, in 2021 and in subsequent years. In particular, it concerns a minimum value for reducing greenhouse gas emissions over the life cycle, inter alia. liquid fuels, liquid biofuels, liquefied LPG gas, CNG compressed natural gas and liquefied LNG gas used in transport per unit of energy. The Commission for Energy, Climate and State Assets, following a detailed examination of the project at its meeting on 6 July 2021, recommends the adoption by the High Sejm of the draft contained in Seym Printing No 1346, in particular that the project is compatible with European Union law and aims to bring the law into line with EU law in this respect. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u154	Thank you very much, Member. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u155	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club Law and Justice a position on the government draft law amending the Law on the Fuel Quality Monitoring and Control System and some other laws aimed at implementing European Union law. The project concerns the implementation of the National Reduction Objective, the NCR, in 2021 and in subsequent years, i.e. the minimum value of reducing greenhouse gas emissions over the life cycle, among others. liquid fuels, liquid biofuels, liquefied LPG gas, CNG compressed natural gas or liquefied LNG gas used in transport per unit of energy. The Act also concerns the abolition of the obligation to report jointly on the implementation of the NCR, the extension of the deadline for the submission of annual reports on the implementation of the NCR, the clarification of the rules on penalties for the joint implementation of the NCR. It is also possible to implement the NCR using instruments in the form of a substitute fee. This law takes into account the change in the definition of light fuel oil and heavy fuel oil, due to the need to adapt them to the changes introduced in the European Union. This is due to a change in the sulphur content of oils. The implementation of the National Reduction Objective shall be carried out by operators themselves or through another operator of the manufacture, import or acquisition of liquid fuels, liquid biofuels, liquefied LPG gas, CNG compressed natural gas, LNG liquefied natural gas or oil for inland waterway engines. Importantly, the draft law responds to the demands and expectations of the industry submitted to the Minister responsible. energy by the animals obliged to implement the NCR, e.g. involved in the production, import or acquisition of internal Community fuels for liquid motor gasoline, diesel. The above mentioned entities indicated in letters addressed to the Minister that it was not possible to implement the NCR at the required level in 2020. At the same time, they presented proposals for legislative changes which would contribute to the implementation of the NCR at a higher level than is possible in the current legal state. The Act extends the obligation to control the quality of fuels with hydrogen, fuel of the future, which is essentially not yet available on the Polish market, but will be used to drive vehicles. This is due to the future importance of this alternative fuel and the protection of the future user. For this reason, a document was introduced in the form of a hydrogen quality certificate. In the course of yesterday's work on the Act in the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets, six legislative or clarification amendments were tabled, which were adopted by the government and the committee. The draft law is compatible with European Union law, which is important, and is not notified. The proposed law will enter into force 14 days after the announcement, except for example. the provision amending the Act - Energy Law, which will enter into force on the day following the date of its publication, with effect from 1 July 2021, and the provisions on hydrogen, the entry into force of which is planned to enter into force on 1 January 2023. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I recommend the adoption of this bill by the High House. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u156	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mrs Mirosława Nykiel, the Civic Coalition.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u185	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! A lot of questions have been repeated. Maybe I'll start by asking the most common questions, which are questions about fuel prices. Ladies and gentlemen, as you well know, and we are all aware of this, fuel prices shape the market. The rise in oil prices on stock exchanges due to the recovery of the post-pandemic economy results in this rather than a different reaction. Hence the rise in fuel prices at petrol stations not only in our country, but also throughout Europe and around the world. This is an economic process. We're all well aware of that. When it comes to the next most frequently asked question, why it is being processed at such a rate, it is a standard, normal rate for us. We don't think this pace is extraordinary. The need on the part of the state is that we make such changes. In this case, the aim is for the legislation to enter into force in 2021, so that the substitute fee can be used by the company. I stress: so that the company can benefit from this. The regulations that concern hydrogen are also expected by our entrepreneurs. Entrepreneurs want to prepare for this. It is about having a legal basis to prepare for some changes and solutions. That's why it's so fast. As far as the element of achieving the national reduction target is concerned, you must know, and this is also an important information, that this is the objective which the Republic has set itself as a very important part of the fight against climate change. Here too I would like to inform you that with this national reduction target in EU countries We're not the only ones with a problem. We are trying to find a good, beneficial solution that will also restrict the use of other financial tools. They can be used instead of a substitute fee by simply purchasing UERs from oil projects. Our companies could benefit from other measures that are intended for such projects, i.e. oil projects. When we introduce a substitute fee, these measures do not go outside, they are not bought, they only go to our cash register, which means they stay in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The entity that will implement this and will collect the resources that we will later use to develop electromobility and alternative fuels is the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. This is therefore a positive solution. The funds do not go abroad, they stay in our country and are used here to reduce pollution. I'll get to the detailed questions. Does Orlen meet the national reduction target? It is difficult for us to answer clearly, because it is commercial information actually, so it is difficult to speak publicly about the secret of the company. Yesterday at the committee meeting, you didn't hear that information either, you know that. I think that, as part of the parliamentary interpellation, you can gain knowledge in this area. Another question. As we refer to the national objective: whether up to 40% or up to 55% ? To answer this question, I would like to say to you that the bill under review refers to 40% of the reduction in emissions in the European Union. A package of directives aimed at implementing this increased to 55% the target will be presented by the European Commission in the near future. I've already answered the question about the price of fuel. As far as increased fuel consumption and fuel quality are concerned, this was another question - and the impact - may I combine this - of a replacement charge on fuel prices on the market, I will try to explain this to you in a brief information. The introduction of this instrument, i.e. a substitute fee, firstly, allows operators to achieve the national reduction target at the level required at the moment. The lack of possibility of using a substitute fee results in a lack of implementation of the national reduction target, which will therefore involve, as I have already said, penalties for this purpose. I must tell you, by the way, that in the European Union, it is a major problem for countries to achieve a national reduction level. Only two EU countries from all EU countries, Sweden and Finland, are implementing a national reduction level. So this is not a problem that only we are facing, the whole of the European Union is dealing with this problem. Entities pursuing the national reduction target already bear the costs of fulfilling this obligation - as I said - by purchasing the EUR or possibly biocomponents that are fuel components. The introduction of an instrument in the form of a substitute fee should in principle not generate - and we assume that there will be - an additional impact on fuel prices at stations, because this is another instrument, let's say, in the basket of all instruments that can be used alternately and which in full, in the package, can also generate some additional costs. On the other hand, in the absence of a national reduction target, we would incur additional costs for the penalties paid. This is what I said at the very beginning, that instead of paying these penalties, these funds will affect us, the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. Therefore, this is an additional advantage, this is not - as you have described here - a tribute or a cash jump, if I quote correctly. In conclusion, what I wanted to say about prices... [[The hydrogen thing.]] Please? [[I asked you about hydrogen.]] I'll get right to it. In conclusion, the issue of prices, I would like to say that you must know that fuel efficiency and consumption now depend on the quality and efficiency of the engines. This is a very important element. In modern engines, modern cars this consumption is less, so we have to pay attention to the fact that it is correlated with old age, that is, with the age of the cars that we have in our country, that is correlated. So that's the reason that we also need to take into account in this discussion. As far as hydrogen is concerned, of course this is the fuel of the future, which has been said several times. With hydrogen we have very high hopes, with development in this area. Therefore, we are already introducing provisions in this law on the possibility of using hydrogen in advance of certain aspects of the development of the hydrogen market. The development of hydrogen is really the development of the entire economy, the whole infrastructure that we need to build from the very beginning is research in this area. By combining answers to all these questions, I would like to say that we have institutes in our country, which are very serious, very professionally engaged in the research of hydrogen, we have knowledge in this field. Instytut Nafty i Gazu - State Research Institute, Institute of Energy - Research Institute are institutions that have a good scientific basis for talking about hydrogen production, how we can use and transfer hydrogen, these institutions do. All these projects are being implemented right now. Of course, they are at the level of producing documentation, and funds are not yet spent in this area. Nevertheless, there are projects of a national environmental fund which finance, for example, a project such as the development of low-carbon transport, already financed cars based on hydrogen or urban transport based on hydrogen. So it's happening, ladies and gentlemen, it's happening and it's going to happen. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Stanisław Bukowiec, Law and Justice, first.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u189	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to recommend to the High House the adoption of a bill amending the Public Finance Act and certain other laws. In 2020, as a result of the amendment of the Law of 27 August 2009 on public finances, adopted by the Act of 28 May 2020 amending the Law on public finances, the conditions allowing the temporary suspension of the stabilisation of the expenditure rule and the application of the so-called "public finance law' were supplemented. an exit clause throughout the country if the economic situation deteriorates significantly. The Act provides that, in the financial year in which certain criteria cease to be met, the so-called "freedom' shall be automatically activated. a return clause that will allow a gradual return to the amount of expenditure resulting from the original formula stabilising the expenditure rule in the horizon from 2 to 4 years. The current wording of the Public Finance Act and the macroeconomic forecasts provide for a return to the application of a stabilising expenditure rule in accordance with the original formula of the article. 112aa Act on public finances already in the second financial year since suspension, i.e. in 2022. In the light of the experience of the 2008 economic crisis and the progress of the current pandemic and its impact on the economy, the criteria on which the original formula for a return to a stabilising expenditure rule after suspension are too restrictive. The European Commission is currently calling for an extension of the general exit clause of the European Union rules until the recovery of GDP to pre-pandemic levels, in order to continue anti-crisis measures and promote economic recovery. The International Monetary Fund also signals the need for further changes to the return clause, since the start of fiscal consolidation since 2022 may be too rapid and rapid. Therefore, amendments to the Article are proposed. 112aa and Art. 112d Public Finance Act on the exit and return clause stabilizing the expenditure rule. The bill presented includes e.g. changes to the exit and return clause stabilizing the expenditure rule, extending the scope of units and bodies covered by the stabilizing expenditure rule, consolidating the flows between units and bodies subject to a stabilizing expenditure rule, or introducing the amount of expenditure taking into account State-owned special-purpose funds. The proposed rules will not directly affect the entire socio-economic life in Poland. However, failure to implement the proposed changes would prevent the economy from supporting additional measures in 2022, as the current formula of the "return clause" would have started fiscal consolidation already in 2022. The extension of the scope of the stabilisation of the expenditure rule is, in turn, one of the obligations contained in the National Plan for Reconstruction, a plan to increase resilience. This document is committed to implementing the relevant legislative changes in the third quarter of 2021 so that new solutions are implemented in the work of the Draft Budget Law for 2022. Covering the rule of more general government units will increase transparency and efficiency of public finance management while maintaining the operationality of the rule. It is also important that the bill does not directly affect micro-entrepreneurs or small and medium-sized enterprises. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the provisions of the bill and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much.

High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, I present a report on a government draft law amending the Real Estate Management Act and some other laws. The draft is contained in print No 1266 and the report of the joint committees is contained in print No 1290. The first reading of the government project took place at the joint committee meeting on 22 June this year. The committees immediately joined the detailed work on the project and, after its work, present this print report no. 1290 to the High House. High Izbo! It seems obvious that the owner of the property knows what he has at his disposal, knows the location, the area, the destination in the spatial planning plan, the value of his property, knows what benefits and income he can obtain from it. However, it turns out that this is not always the case. In view of the property of the State Treasury, which is managed by seniors as part of government tasks and which represent several percent of the national assets, there are no legal instruments to ensure that voivodships exercise supervision and control of their proper management. This supervision can be considered to be largely illusory, because, in the absence of cooperation from the old-timer, who keeps records of land and buildings, the voivode does not even know about the real estate stock, but let's talk about checking the regularity of the economy with this resource and realizing the interests of the State Treasury. On the other hand, the management of public immovable property should be efficient and flexible, without unnecessary mitraga, without unnecessary costs. E.g. When the voivodship gives its consent to rent, loan, lease for more than 3 years, i.e. exceeding the scope of the ordinary board, there is no need to present the current estimate of which, after all, it costs by the qualified valuer, and it is only necessary in this case to assess the amount of the proposed rent, which can be presented without estimation of the property. It is also necessary to amend the provisions on public donations for the property of the State Treasury where this objective cannot be achieved for reasons independent of the gifted one. High Izbo! The bill discussed here amends the Act of 21 August 1997 on real estate management, the Act of 19 October 1991 on the management of agricultural property of the State Treasury, the Act of 23 January 2009 on voivodeship and government administration in the voivodship and the Act of 16 December 2016 on the rules for the management of state property. All these changes have received a positive opinion from the Commission of the Joint Government and the Territorial Government. The changes to the rules contained in this project give the voivodeship the right to request information from senior property managers and other bodies about state property. They also give the right to act on behalf of the Treasury in situations where the governor does not take them in spite of his rights and adequate financial resources for the tasks entrusted. The Starosta will be required to transfer the property on request contained in the request from the voivode or the Minister for the transfer of the property to the purpose indicated in the request. High Izbo! A few more brief information about the detailed solutions contained in the project, which are introduced into the four laws mentioned by me, for obvious reasons. This is, among others, the case. clarification of the costs of using the property incurred by the permanent management body. I mean annual fees and conversion fees. Another issue is to allow an organizational unit, which includes a permanent management of real estate, to enter into a civil-law relationship established by the predecessor. This is certainly a big simplification and facilitation. Another issue is the exemption from the obligation to present a valuation of real estate. I already told you there's a problem with that. This is a source of unnecessary expenditure. Therefore, there is an exemption from the obligation to present a valuation of the property when applying for authorisation to lease, rent or loan the property, and to provide copies of the perpetual accounts for the property in question with an appropriate application for authorisation to carry out the transaction. Another issue is the release of the Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing from the obligation to keep central records of the State Treasury's real estate and to allow the Minister to speak to the relevant entities keeping records of these properties. The project is also carrying out such a thing as restoring the obligation for the starosts to conduct a plan for the use of the resource and control of the voivodships over the implementation of these plans. The project also allows the old man to take action within a certain period of time on the specific property of the State Treasury, and when the old man fails to do so, it enables the voivode to take these actions. The last point I would like to mention concerns the possibility of amending the terms of the contract for making a donation for its proper development. In short, such issues are consummated in this project. High Izbo! During the work of the joint committees, thirty-six amendments proposed by the Legislative Bureau were introduced in the draft law. They were editorial, legislative. This consisted in explaining some doubts to the Legislative Bureau. The joint report by the Commission: Infrastructure and Regional Government and Regional Policy on the draft law presented was adopted by 30 votes in favour, with one abstention and one vote against. On behalf of the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Committee on Infrastructure, I ask the High House to adopt the draft presented in the report of the Joint Committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u225	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the government draft law amending the Real Estate Management Act and some other laws, print No 1266. The proposed changes result from the need to improve public property management mechanisms, ensure a sound economy, maintain special care and strengthen supervision of the activities undertaken in relation to the property of the State Treasury. In their current legal status, the State Treasury's real estate resources are managed, subject to the exceptions provided for in the Real Estate Management Act and certain other laws, by seniors carrying out tasks in the field of government administration. The voivodships supervise the activities undertaken by the seniors in the area of real estate management. The conclusions and audit observations of the Supreme Audit Chamber following ad hoc checks in the area of treasury management demonstrate the necessity of introducing solutions to strengthen and support the supervisory activities of the voivodships in carrying out the tasks contracted by seniors and to take legislative action in this area. The conclusions of the Supreme Audit Chamber show the need for supervision and control of all tasks in the field of government administration, which have been commissioned to local government units, to ensure that the elderly carry out checks on the performance of the duties related to the management of the Treasury's real estate, to use all possible means of supervision to carry out the tasks resulting from the disclosure law in the books of perpetual property rights of the State Treasury and local governments, including the enforcement of the obligation to submit reports. The Supreme Audit Chamber assessed as ineffective the supervision by the voivodships in 2011 and 2013 of the implementation by local government units of the tasks entrusted with the management of the State Treasury's real estate which is the subject of control. In the NIK's assessment, subsequent audits have consistently shown that it is necessary to change the approach to the role of voivodships in the area of supervision of the performance of outsourced tasks, in particular as regards the development of the State Treasury's real estate. The conclusions and audit observations of the NIK show that the search for solutions to strengthen the supervision of the voivodships over the actions of the elders is necessary. The solutions proposed in the new law aim to give voivodships legal tools and strong supervisory powers to eliminate these irregularities resulting from the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber. This will be supported by the following solutions proposed in the project. Making plans for the use of the State Treasury's real estate stock, which will be approved by the voivodships, which will ensure proper, planned management of the State Treasury's real estate and reliable information about the state and structure of the stock. This will translate into real, deliberate and systematic supervision of the voivodships on the implementation of planned actions. Expansion of competence in the acquisition of real estate data by voivodships, including on the basis of IT tools and electronic data flow. The data obtained will allow the voivodships to assess the performance of the task commissioned and will also be able to verify the data contained in the resource use plans. The obligation of the old-timer to take certain actions within the prescribed time limits for the specific property of the stock and, in the event of default by the old-timer, to take these actions on behalf of the Treasury. The obligation of the old-timer to transfer the property of the Treasury's stock for the purpose specified by the voivode in the application. The solutions proposed in the project are also part of the proposals made by the voivodships for the introduction of legal instruments enabling voivodships to exercise effective and reliable control over the management of the property of the State Treasury by the elderly. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I strongly recommend the above-mentioned bill. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I am making amendments to the bill. These are four amendments. Their aim is to eliminate interpretational doubts, as well as in Amendment 3, to propose the wording of a provision which will enable the elderly to express their views also on the property which the voivod has requested before the amendment of that provision. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u255	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to begin by thanking all the members of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy very warmly for an in-depth analysis of the project, a fruitful discussion of the proposed solutions and comments. In particular, I would like to thank the legislators of the Legislative Office, whose constructive comments have contributed to clarifying the provisions of the project in a manner consistent with the principles of legislative technique. The bill submitted for amending the Real Estate Management Act and some other laws was constructed on the basis of the requests of government and local government authorities resulting from the practice of applying the rules. Some of the proposed solutions concern not only the property of the State Treasury, but also the property of local governments, so they represent a good cooperation and consensus that was achieved in agreements with local governments. However, the vast majority of the proposed regulations concern a relatively small stock of State Treasury real estate, which is estimated at a maximum of several percent of the total property of the State Treasury. The project therefore does not affect the management of a larger part of the State Treasury's real estate stock, which is forest, agricultural, defensive or water-occupied land. The principles of the management of these land are governed by other laws. What is the purpose of the revised law? The amendment of the law is aimed primarily at rationalising and improving the processes of managing the property of the State Treasury. This objective will be achieved through the legal instruments envisaged in the draft, first of all, strengthening cooperation between voivods and starosts in the public property management process - one of the solutions proposed to strengthen this cooperation is to base the management of the Treasury's resources on plans to use the stock drawn up by the starosts. The exchange of information on the property of the State Treasury will be improved - the proposed solutions will enable the Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing to quickly obtain information on the property of the State Treasury necessary for the effective execution of tasks from various bodies with this information. The project will then help to deformise the procedure for the voivodship's approval of the State Treasury's property and to stabilise the legal situation of tenants, tenants or beneficiaries on the basis of lease agreements made to the permanent management. In addition, the project grants the voivodships instruments that will enable effective implementation of tasks to ensure the proper management of the property of the State Treasury and will make the best use of the investment potential of these properties, as well as contribute to maintaining the property owned by the State Treasury in good condition. The emphasis is on the fact that the project was positively assessed by the Joint Committee of the Government and the Local Government, and the provisions of the project were made in a compromise manner, taking into account the comments of the local government, including the Polish Districts Association. To answer the questions that have been raised during today's debate on this project, I will start with issues related to the purpose of the donation. The flexibility of the provisions on real estate donations from the Treasury resource will strengthen the deliberate nature of the use of these properties. The decision provided for in the draft body to change the purpose of the donation will be taken within the ownership powers and will mainly be dictated by the actual use of the property. There are situations where the gifted cannot, for various objective reasons, pursue the previously intended purpose, but in the course of his business, for example. public benefit could pursue another socially useful objective. In such cases, the Authority will be able to change the target to make optimal use of the property. Of course, information about the real estate that will be held by the authority will be necessary to make such a decision, but it will also be possible to ask other entities to do so. On the other hand, between public resources, because such a statement has been made, the purpose of the donation may, by nature, be more flexible, because public authorities only pursue public objectives, so there is no risk of being used for any other purpose, which is not foreseen by the provisions of the Real Estate Management Act. With regard to the standards of professional property assessors, it should be pointed out that no longer the standards developed by the professional environment are binding on the drawing up of valuations. They are good practices, methodological guidelines that help assessors in their daily work, but are not mandatory. Nevertheless, they have been widely used by experts for many years. This isn't gonna change. The proposed amendment concerns only a body which sets standards and which is currently a minister. We want to give up imposing a work workshop on valuers by a state authority. A system of universal law should not be a place to regulate such issues. Professional standards are, by their very nature, industry standards with a high degree of specialisation, which is why it is optimal to leave them to create and improve their professional environment. In the past, such a solution was successful for many years before 2004. We want to point out here that we are not changing the valuation rules. The valuation rules shall be governed by a separate regulation and the law in question shall in no way amend that Regulation. As regards the questions concerning Article. 58a and possible e-interference in the actions of starosts, the proposed regulations do not modify the competence for managing the property of the State Treasury by the starosts performing it as part of the commissioned task. The changes merely clarify the procedure for dealing with conflict situations, which it turns out, in practice sometimes occur on the starost line - the voivode and cause an impasse. The solution to this problem is the result of a compromise with the local government, which was expressed in the positive opinion of the Commission of the Joint Government and the Territorial Government. These rules take into account the votes of the districts opting for the possibility of expressing opinions and taking enforcement action against the property of the State Treasury. In the event of a dispute over the management of the property, the voivode will ultimately decide, taking into account the opinion of the old man. The need for a clear definition of the scope and manner of implementation of the voivodship's supervisory powers was pointed out by NIK in the course of several inspections carried out in this area, as detailed in the justification for this project. It should be strongly emphasised, however, that the transfer of property to the voivode does not entail any changes in the ownership sphere, but only modifies the jurisdiction of the body managing the property of the State Treasury. With regard to the comments on the plan for the use of resources, including immovable property for perpetual use, it should be noted that the amendment still does not introduce any new obligation in the area of obtaining information on land for perpetual use. Already on the basis of the current provision of the article. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1b Act on Real Estate Management The obligation to register real estate of the State Treasury also applies to those properties which have been given in perpetual use. Currently, the starosts are obliged to display the area, the destination of the property as well as the date of the last update of the annual fee. These are the information necessary for the purpose and rational management of the State Treasury's real estate. The concept of a rational economy also includes the taking of economically justified actions for the owner. Hence, knowledge of the amount of income that real estate brings in perpetual use is of course essential. It should also be clarified that plans for the use of the State Treasury's real estate stock will be drawn up on the basis of plans already drawn up for the use of municipal, district and voivodship real estate resources. At present, these plans also show land for perpetual use, even though these are non-resource real estate, as they have been permanently disposed of for a total period of 99 years. With regard to questions related to the KZN Act, the Real Estate Management Act does not regulate the transfer of land to KZN. This amended provision does not apply at all to the donation procedure for KZN, KZN is governed by a separate law. With regard to questions and doubts concerning the application of Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4b and the application of excessive financial burdens on permanent managers with subsequent fees is our position that the rule merely expresses the practice of most permanent managers for years. Permanent administrators pursuant to Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Real Estate Management Act acquires real estate for the property or in the perpetual use of the State Treasury, obtaining permanent management of the property by virtue of law. It is therefore difficult to assume that the State Treasury would be liable for perpetual use in a situation where it does not affect the decisions to acquire that right. The right of perpetual use acquires the entity for its statutory purposes and, therefore, when deciding to acquire it, provides financial resources in its budget to cover the maintenance costs of the property, including public-law liabilities. With regard to issues related to whether we grant voivodships too much competence in the area of supervision of the oldships, it must be said that the basis for regulating the relationship between the voivodship and the senior in the management of the property of the State Treasury are multiple audit recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber, which indicated that, above all, the supervision of the voivodship over the property of the State Treasury is illusory, and that the voivodships have often not even kept records of the properties they used to manage, so many shortcomings have already been indicated in the control recommendations of the Supreme Auditing Chamber. The voivodship's expenditures do not take into account the law because, according to the proposed project, they will not increase. It should be borne in mind that in the current legal state, the voivodship's role is to supervise the management of the property of the State Treasury by the elderly. As NIK pointed out, supervision is so far illusory and in this respect we do not change the rules, but we make it realized, realized and most of all improved. It should also be borne in mind that, with the strengthening of the role of the voivodship, greater responsibility is placed on it when it comes to the management of the State Treasury's real estate. So this is the argument that responsibility is transferred to an old woman, it is missed, because it is imposed on the voivode and it is on the voivodship that is more responsible for giving instructions in this regard. I think I've answered all the questions because some of the questions have been answered several times. Then thank you very much for your attention. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you very much. Undersecretary of State of the Ministry of Development, Labour and Technology Anna Kornecka: Maybe I'll just ask for a bill, possibly with these editorial amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u259	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, I present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Cleanliness and Order in municipalities and certain other laws. Print project 1275 and print report 1295. Both committees to which your Marshal has addressed this project in accordance with Article. 37 and 40 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm held a joint meeting on 22 June of the year in which the first reading of the draft was carried out. The committees then joined the detailed examination of the project, which resulted in the report being presented. High Izbo! The print project No 1275 amends the Cleanliness and Order Act in municipalities, the Environmental Law and the Waste Act. The provisions on the collection and management of municipal waste have been amended several times in recent years, with an important change being made 2 years ago by the Law of 19 July 2019. The changes adopted at that time are strengthening the supervision of municipalities of waste collectors, incentives for selective collection of municipal waste, including biodegradable, demonopoliation of the market for the collection of municipal waste, i.e. abolishing the regionalisation of waste management, simplifying the rules for building waste treatment facilities by local authorities. High Izbo! This was 2 years ago, but in order for these changes to produce the expected results and to reduce the charges paid by residents, they should be introduced into the municipal cleanliness and order regulations. The cost of municipal waste management can be reduced after a reliable introduction by local authorities recommended by the European Union of the waste hierarchy. To put it simply, it is about selective collection, reuse of glass, metal, plastic and other suitable waste, thermal conversion of waste with energy recovery, so as to deposit in landfills only what is absolutely impossible to use again. Unfortunately, municipalities sometimes do not respect the clearly expressed intention of the legislator. The elimination of regionalisation of waste management has been used e.g. by one of the big cities to carry unsegregated waste in Podkarpat or near Białystok, disrupting local waste markets, which in addition to bad results in high charges for residents, because driving garbage around the country costs. On the other hand, it is heard that the costs generated by real estate have not been passed on to municipal waste management systems. An example from my constituency: in one of the famous tourist towns of Podhale the hotel owner checked in and paid 16 PLN a month times 12 months, i.e. 192 zlotys a year. However, the cost of disposing of waste containers from its premises is 37 000 a year. zł. This is not a good situation, this situation should be changed, because because because of the money of other taxpayers, the municipality has to pay for such activities. [[Madam, report!]] The Mayors and Mayors are asking for more flexibility in the charging system and for more detail in this respect. High Izbo! One of the changes proposed in this government bill is among others. increase of the maximum fee for uninhabited properties. That's the problem I was talking about, the hotel is just an uninhabited property. Another issue is the introduction of provisions allowing the owner to make a declaration by the owner of the uninhabited real estate about excluding himself from the municipal waste management system. This provision will make it easier for municipalities to determine the size of the contract, because the municipality must conduct tenders for both residents and uninhabited properties in order to select the contractor. This way of making statements will therefore make it easier for the municipalities to determine the size of the contract and to conduct tender procedures for the export and management of municipal waste. Another issue that solves this project is to enable owners of recreational and summer property to settle their fees, such as owners of uninhabited properties, i.e. from the container. There is also provision for individual settlement of residents in multi-site buildings from a selective collection of waste, provided that the municipality provides technical possibilities to identify waste generated in individual premises in such a way that unauthorised persons cannot read personal data. There is a chance that this solution will achieve a higher level of selective collection. It is clear that if there are many residents in the block and if there is at least one that does not segregate, the rest loses the motivation to conduct a selective collection, and yet the selective collection should be carried out at the earliest possible stage, at the source of the waste. Another point I would like to mention here is to allow municipalities to cover part of the cost of managing waste from their own income, which does not come from the charges levied on residents for the management of municipal waste. High Izbo! Another problem which is also addressed in the project is the possibility for the Minister responsible for Climate Affairs to determine, by decision, derogations for individual municipalities for the selective collection of municipal waste, in accordance with Article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Directive 2008/98 of the European Parliament and of the Council on waste, provided, of course, that the criteria set out in the Act are met. In short, the point is that if a municipality meets three of the four criteria indicated in the Act, it is possible to request a derogation. Often field conditions hinder the collection, render it ineffective, cause excessive costs. The aim is to react flexibly, adapting to the specific conditions that occur in a particular municipality. Another issue, please, is the introduction of a requirement for municipalities to first ensure that waste collected selectively is prepared for reuse and recycling, and that waste generated in the sorting process reduces waste management costs. High Izbo! All these issues were discussed in very detail during the work of the Joint Committee on the project. Of course, amendments of an editorial, legislative nature have been introduced. The Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Committee on the Environment of Natural Resources and Forestry have adopted this report. On behalf of the joint committees, I ask the High House to adopt the bill contained in the report of the joint committees in print no. 1295. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u261	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on print no. 1275, i.e. the government draft law amending the Law on Cleanliness and Order in municipalities and some other laws. The proposed amendment is due to the need to support local government units on the problems identified and to improve the situation on the domestic municipal waste management market. The proposals contained in the draft aim, among others, at: solving some of the current problems that are reported by local authorities or by residents that have arisen in recent months, including. This concerns the high costs of municipal municipal waste management systems, the lack of adequate flexibility in shaping municipal waste collection systems, the clarification of the rules on the charging of waste management charges for residents or the high costs of the management of non-combustible waste, as well as the introduction of a maximum limit on the charge for municipal waste management calculated by the method from the amount of water used. The project includes provisions that will reduce the costs of the municipality and municipal waste management systems and improve the system by increasing the maximum fee for non-inhabited properties, introducing provisions to allow the municipal waste management system to be excluded from the EU by owners of non-inhabited property, or to account for the charges paid by owners of recreational and summer property, as for non-inhabited property owners, i.e. from the container, as well as to allow municipalities to cover part of the cost of municipal waste management from their own income, which does not come from the charges collected from residents. Provisions are also introduced to allow individual settlement of residents in multi-family buildings. Ww. The proposals for changes will enable municipalities to apply solutions that can reduce the cost of receiving and managing municipal waste. The changes proposed in the draft will allow, among others. a more flexible implementation by the municipality of the responsibilities for selective collection of waste. The introduction of rules allowing the individual settlement of residents in multi-site buildings from the obligation to collect waste separately will enable the municipality to depart from the so-called. collective responsibility. It can also be expected that residents will start to comply more strictly with the selective collection of waste, which will result in more quality secondary raw materials through collection by municipalities as well as higher levels of recycling and preparation of collected waste for reuse. These factors will reduce the cost of waste management for residents. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I also make amendments to this law and, on behalf of the club, request that the law be adopted together with the amendments tabled. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Firstly, it is the introduction of an extended producer responsibility system, in short the ROP. It is a system in which the manufacturer of the product is responsible for it for the rest of his life, i.e. until it is disposed of. What does that mean? This means that the producer should cover waste disposal costs, which will motivate him to ecodesign products and services. This will reduce the amount of trash, because we will produce less trash. Secondly, it is the introduction of a deposit system for beverage packaging: glass bottles, plastic bottles, aluminium cans. Such systems operate in most European countries. Why haven't we been able to implement this system for years? Because our government is simply incompetent and doesn't do what it needs to do, because if a plastic bottle had value, it would have gone into recycling, not into the woods or into the oven. Thirdly, it is the implementation of the EU SUP directive, namely the single use plastic, which was intended to cause the withdrawal from the use of certain disposable plastic products, e.g. Ear sticks, stir-ups, straws. This Directive was already adopted in 2017 and was to be implemented by July this year at the latest, i.e. the deadline has expired. Most countries have dealt with it, and Poland has what? Nothing at all, nothing at all. There's not even a proposal to change the law. Of course these changes aren't simple. All systemic changes require effort, planning, organization, implementation of new procedures and education. We evaluate and decide how to solve this long-term problem. This is indeed a very important matter that you have signaled. Another issue concerns the protection of Polish families. This is the regulation that really is the result of under-precision of the laws governing the four methods. This is a really gigantic project that needs to be worked out over divisions. That is why, of course, I am addressing all those concerned who want to work on this. Once we have developed a concept, we will be open to further discussion. Today, a legislative response is needed primarily to optimise costs or cost factors such as the calorie fraction. The availability of incinerators and the prohibition of storage caused this price to go giganticly up. This, of course, affects the cost to the residents. Therefore, we extend the storage period from one year to three years. It is not that we are deregulating the issue of selective collection. The Directive allows for derogation if there are technical and organisational possibilities. While we are still adding some elements to secure this, if there is a local government whose inhabitants will approach it more innovatively, and there will be an opportunity that will give an environmental effect, a level effect that results from the directives, such a local government may ask the Ministry for approval. We estimate that a few of these places might be in the country. So why should we take something that gives us a directive? I understand that you are still one step ahead of the European Union.
