ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u337	Thank you very much again. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the Commission's draft law amending the Law on State Labour Inspection, prints No 1088 and 1367. The project envisages granting labour inspectors, i.e. employees carrying out control tasks and having at least 10 years' experience in the positions involved in the performance of these activities, the right to additional leave of 6 working days. For inspectors with an internship of over 20 years - 12 working days. It should be stressed here that similar powers are also available, inter alia. Inspectors of the Supreme Audit Chamber, judges, curators and prosecutors. We therefore accept this solution as a harmonisation of the provisions of the Act on State Labour Inspection with other similar institutions and bodies in which control activities are carried out. I would like to emphasise the role and importance of the work carried out by PIP inspectors, which is extremely difficult, responsible and very often dangerous, as the annual reports of the institution show. It is they who are often the first people to be in contact with the threat of life and health of the employed, who intervene and, as a result, take appropriate action to break labour law, to break the law on technical safety at work, the legality of employment and many other areas in this area. The State Labour Inspection is a supervisory and control body with a beautiful, more than 100-year-old tradition. It's an office of trust and respect, and every single inspector's work is invaluable. As former head of these wonderful people, I am pleased to inform you that the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this proposed bill that is expected by the inspector environment. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u338	Thank you very much, Member. Now I ask Mrs Isabella Catherine Mrzygłocka to present the position of the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u339	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition - Civic Platform, I have the honour to present an opinion on the draft law amending the Act on State Labour Inspection, contained in the sejm print No. 1088. The project is laconic, but very important. Predicts after the article. 52 introduction of Article. 52a, on the basis of which the staff of the State Labour Inspection are granted, having at least 10 years' experience in the post of inspection, the right to additional leave. Thus, in the course of an internship of not less than 10 years, it is 6 working days, and after 20 years it is 12 working days. The new rules introduce special arrangements for holiday leave for employees of the State Labour Inspection. At this point, I would like to point out that such solutions are already available to employees of another control body, namely the Supreme Audit Chamber, as well as to judges and prosecutors. The proposed provisions should enter into force on 1 January 2022 in view of the settlement of holiday leave during the calendar year cycle. The project is the result of a petition by the Workers' Union of the State Labour Inspection to the Committee on Petitions, which is its author. In the process of this project, it should be stressed that the proposed solutions are an expression of appreciation for the staff of the State Labour Inspection performing control activities. It must be borne in mind that the work of the State Labour Inspection inspectors is very difficult and stressful. It requires very high psychological and physical resilience, so these additional vacation days will help to regenerate the forces that are necessary for further work and for carrying out further checks. In conclusion, the proposed solution deserves support as an expression of appreciation and thanks to labour inspectors for their daily efforts, as well as their commitment to ensuring respect for workers' rights. It is also an equalization of the powers of the National Labour and NIK inspectors. The Civic Coalition - Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports these solutions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u373	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! Minister! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the parliamentary draft law amending the Act - Water Law, print no. 1281 and 1363. On 18 June 2021, the Marshal of the Sejm addressed the above draft law to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation to its first reading. The aim of the project is to clarify the formal requirements to be met by applicants for water law permits for the introduction of industrial waste water containing substances harmful to the aquatic environment into sewage systems. Here's the important thing. The implementation of this law will not have any additional financial implications and it is therefore not appropriate to indicate the sources of funding. It is quite important, such a social but also desirable issue, that the amendment of the law will have a positive impact on the activities of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, since the proposed clarification will simplify the process of obtaining water permits in this area in the absence of the need to obtain a separate statement of agreement in the event that the owner of the plumbing equipment has in fact given such consent by concluding an appropriate contract. This is very important because many small entrepreneurs, but also medium-sized and large, were waiting for this bill. The proposed law will have the desired social impact by means of this clarification. The draft does not provide for authorisations to issue implementing acts. The subject matter of the proposed regulation is not covered by EU law, so it is a typical law, matter, which must be simply clarified. The Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, following its first reading at its meeting on 23 June, considered this draft law at its meeting on 6 July and gave a positive opinion. On behalf of the committee, I ask the High House to adopt the draft Print Act No 1281 without amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u377	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendment to the Act - Water Law is, as stated in the explanatory memorandum, a clarification of the formal requirements for documents attached to the application for a water and legal permit for the introduction of industrial sewage systems containing substances which are particularly harmful to the aquatic environment. The formal condition is to be met both by providing a declaration of acceptance of the owner of the sewage treatment facilities and by concluding a contract with such owner. This amendment is intended to facilitate the obtaining of a water and legal permit in this respect by operators using third party plumbing facilities. In relation to this law, concerns were raised by the Polish Water Supply Chamber, indicating that the water and sewage company should not be allowed to lose the possibility of negotiating contracts with waste water suppliers. So it can't, for example. specify the conditions for the reception of waste water so that it cannot impose requirements, e.g. prior to the introduction of these waste water into the sewerage facilities, they were cleaned. It is not possible to deprive water and sewage undertakings of the impact on who and under what conditions or with what objections waste water will be received. On the other hand, twice during two committee meetings, the Ministry of Infrastructure tried to table an amendment that was devoid of such influence. Luckily, it was withdrawn. But even these trials themselves show worrying efforts to turn upside down the law governing water and law permits and waste water management. This is in a situation where we have to make it clear that supervision of waste water management in many municipalities is incorrect and ineffective. This is alerted in numerous inspections by the Supreme Audit Chamber, but above all also by residents who report pollution of waters, illegal discharges, accidents in overloaded networks or sewage treatment plants to voivodship environmental inspectors. Businesses and industrial waste water are also a problem, in which the treatment process is very complex and which burden often requiring the modernisation of outdated sewage systems. In such a situation, we should rather do our utmost to modernise sewerage networks and equipment, while the law should be restrictive in order to protect people and the environment. However, this fact shows that there is much to be done here. I think this House should work on this first and foremost. It should be the purpose of the government to actually protect the environment in Poland, and it was not a fig leaf, and it would hide that it is very bad in Poland with the state of water, with sewage, with sewage management. Thank you.

The proposed regulation was developed following a review of the existing provisions of the Act of 15 September 2000 on groups of agricultural producers and their associations and on the amendment of other laws. It responds to the requests made by those involved in the process of setting up groups of agricultural producers and groups themselves on problems and doubts regarding the application of the provisions of the abovementioned regulation. Laws. The amendments are intended to clarify some of the current provisions of the 2000 Act in order to avoid interpretation doubts. Amendments proposed in Article Points 2 and 3 of Article 1 3 Laws of 2000 reduce the mandatory level of sales volume from 80 to 70% production produced by a member of the group. This change will make it more flexible to use parts of production e.g. a plant on the holding of a member of the group. Much of the changes concern simplification, flexibility of administrative procedures. The committee members in the general discussion on the project raised the problem of very small organisation of Polish farmers in producer groups or other organisational forms, e.g. cooperatives. We're talking 1% to 2%. farmers, so any facilitation in organizing is highly desirable. No amendments were tabled by the members of the committee during the detailed examination of the draft law. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u393	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In Poland, within the framework of the Act on groups of agricultural producers, acceptable legal forms have been narrowed down to commercial law companies, cooperatives, associations and associations. Currently registered groups of agricultural producers are 886, in which some 12.5 thousand cooperate. Members. The most are groups active in the cereals and oil seeds sector, pigs or milk producers. At this stage, farmers can work together in the form of groups of agricultural producers. The proposed amendments to the Act of 15 September 2000 on groups of agricultural producers and their associations and the amendment of other laws respond to requests made by operators involved in the process of setting up producer groups and the producer groups themselves as regards the application of the provisions cited above. Laws and which supervise groups, in particular as amended by the Act of 11 September 2015 amending the Law on groups of agricultural producers and their associations and amending other laws and the Law on support for rural development with the participation of the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development in the framework of the RDPs for the years 2014 to 2020 and aim to clarify certain provisions of the Act of 15 September 2000. And so on. Amendment to Article Point 1 of the draft is an order change to indicate that the provisions of the Law of 14 June 1960 apply to individual proceedings in the form of decisions. k.p.a., unless otherwise stated in the Act. It is proposed to amend the provisions of the Act of 15 September 2000 to reduce the mandatory level of volume produced by a member of a group of agricultural producers of products or groups of products for which the group was set up to be sold to the group from at least 80% to at least 70% at the same time covering at least 90% of the obligation Grouped producers instead of 100% What it's like right now. Meeting the current requirement of 80% of sales to producer groups poses serious difficulties especially for members of cereal producer groups. Farmers producing a plant-animal profile allocate part of their production to feed or seed and this purpose, 80% It was difficult to realize. The introduction of a 90-day deadline for the decision by the director of the regional branch of the agency to recognise the group or approve the business plan and register the group allows the agency to check eligibility requirements, verify and approve the business plan and carry out on-the-spot checks before the group is recognised. The introduction of an obligation to submit together with a request to recognise the group's electronic version of the business plan and the statements made by the members of the group of non-members of the producer group or producer organisations set up for the same product or product group will facilitate and facilitate the work of the groups and agencies. The next major change is the introduction of a procedure whereby, if a group of agricultural producers found irregularities in compliance with the recognition criteria applicable to the head office of the Agency, the Director would call on the group to remedy the irregularity within a specified period of not more than 12 months following the end of the year of activity of the group in which the infringement was found. This amendment will enable the group to remedy the irregularities without the need to initiate administrative proceedings for the withdrawal of recognition by the Agency. Extension of the deadline for the submission by the producer group - or producer organisation - of the application for approval of changes to the business plan at no later date than 60 days before the end of the year in question, the group will not put the producer group at risk of sanctions or withdrawal of recognition. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u397	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the parliamentary club of the Left in relation to the government draft law amending the Act on groups of agricultural producers and their associations and amending other laws, prints No 1343 and 1359. The proposed amendments are purely technical. Examples are the reduction of the mandatory level of agricultural products produced by the members of the group, the extension of time limits when approving the business plan or when registering groups of agricultural producers, etc., etc. These are needed changes, but they don't solve real problems, and these are really deep. I'll try to give them a brief introduction. There are usually several intermediaries between the farmer and the store which have a significant impact on the final selling prices of agricultural products. For example, for sold vegetables and fruits food producers receive small amounts, in extreme cases it is only a dozen percent of the retail price. Most of the profits remain in the hands of intermediaries and large commercial networks, while farmers make disproportionately little money compared to their work and risks. The reasons for this are systemic and fall within the supply chain structure and in the fact that farmers are scattered and do not come together in producer groups. In Poland, only 1% farmers participate in various forms of collective interaction of agricultural producers and are most often small, several-member groups. For comparison e.g. in Denmark, the percentage of farmers' participation in producer groups is close to 100% , in France - 85% , in the Netherlands - 90%. Producer groups may take the organisational form of cooperatives, limited liability companies, associations and associations of agricultural organisations. Currently in Poland there are 886 groups of agricultural producers, including 12.5 thousand. members, so on average there are 14 members in one producer group. Since 2001, the number of producer groups has steadily increased until 2013 and then a slight decrease has been recorded in 2014 and 2016 and the pace of which has clearly accelerated in 2017. Thus, there has been a trend which shows that there are systemic barriers to the functioning of groups. Similarly, the number of group members may even be a better indicator. Is this bill the answer to these problems? No, I'm afraid not. The draft law addresses some minor organisational issues related to the functioning of the groups, but it will not result in a significant improvement in the situation in this area. Perhaps it will stop the downward trend, but let's not fool ourselves: 1% Associated farmers are still 1%. And even if we reach 2%, it still doesn't solve the problems. What is lacking in this project is, on the one hand, the elimination of barriers, such as. legal instability and, on the other hand, real legal, organisational and financial support for creating new and stimulating the growth of existing groups and strengthening their economic strength. Experts, as well as the producer groups themselves, show that the most effective from the point of view of openness, division of power and responsibilities, decision-making, etc. There's a cooperative. This form of organisation should therefore be subject to special support and preferences. We need a systematic approach to this issue, including existing producer groups, especially cooperatives and farmers themselves, in order to meet their needs and to develop the best solutions. Because our goal should be to achieve an association of, say, 30% of farmers at a certain time, not to double the existing number, which is to reach 2%. In conclusion, this bill is a slice for a large wound. The Left will support it, but we recommend that we take it seriously, because the slice here is not a solution enough. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u399	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Coalition Poland - Polish People's Party, Union of European Democrats, Conservatives, I have the honour to present the position of the club regarding the Act amending the Act on groups of agricultural producers and their associations and amending other laws. The proposed amendments to the Act of 11 September 2015 aim to align the existing regulations with the current legal situation as regards the recognition and supervision of groups of agricultural producers and the existing provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure. The proposed regulations respond to the requests made by those involved in the process of setting up groups of agricultural producers and the groups of agricultural producers themselves concerning problems and doubts regarding the application of the rules governing their relevant issues. The most important change in this amendment concerns sales within groups of agricultural producers. The new regulations provide for a reduction in the mandatory volume of products or product groups to be sold to a group of agricultural producers from at least 80% to at least 70%. At least 90% will be covered by this obligation agricultural producers in groups instead of 100% so far. These amendments also concern decisions and e-formalities. A 90-day deadline was introduced for ARiM's directors to issue a decision on the recognition of groups of agricultural producers and to approve their business plans and to enter groups in the register of groups. Another change is the possibility of submitting documents electronically, which is currently very expected by the environment of producer groups and their associations. In addition, an obligation to submit, together with an application for recognition of a group of agricultural producers or producer organisations, an electronic business plan was introduced. The contested law also provides for a number of facilities for the business plan. E.g. the deadline for the submission of an application for approval in the business plan by a group of agricultural producers has been extended. Another proposal is to exclude the association of agricultural producer groups from the obligation to submit the annual accounts to the President of the ARiMR. There will also be a possibility to change the business plan for producer organisations and a request to change the plan should be made at no later date. than 60 days before the end of the year of activity of the producer organisation concerned. AryMR's directors were also authorised to approve changes in business plans at the request of the producer organisation. This request must be submitted no later than 30 days before the end of the year of operation of the organisation concerned, while the provisions concerning the period of termination of membership of the organisation shall be laid down in the statutes, contracts or other instruments of incorporation of the agricultural producer organisation. Joint action for agricultural producers is particularly important in the context of the large fragmentation of agricultural holdings in Poland. For this reason, the negotiating power of a single farm in relation to the processing or trade sector is negligible. The involvement of farmers in groups of agricultural producers provides a basis for the stability of agricultural production and gives the possibility to obtain more favourable conditions for the supply of inputs and sales of manufactured products. In Poland, only about 15% of cooperatives are associated with different types of cooperatives. farmers. The exception is the milk sector, where 75% are brought together. the market. As far as milk is concerned, there are 97% in Denmark. , in the Netherlands - 86% , in Poland mentioned 75% farmers. As far as cereals are concerned, there are 74% in France. farmers in Austria - 70% , in the Netherlands - 60%. As far as sugar is concerned, there are 62% in France. farmers in the Netherlands - 100% , in Austria - 95%. I hope this bill will encourage them to re-establish such groups. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It consists of participating in statutory meetings of bodies and working groups of such organisations, organising conferences and congresses. This serves to exchange views and appoint positions. Membership in transnational agricultural organisations makes it compulsory to pay high membership fees as well as to bear other costs associated with service in Polish. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u423	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Coalition Poland - Polish People's Party, Union of European Democrats, Conservatives I have the honour to present the position of the club regarding the government draft law amending the Act on Agricultural Chambers and some other laws. High Izbo! The role of agricultural chambers, trade unions and agricultural organizations is beyond overestimated in creating the state of Polish agriculture. The National Council of Agricultural Chambers has been representing the interests of hosts from all over Poland for years. The organisation shall regularly issue official letters on behalf of farmers to the Minister for Agriculture with proposals for legislative changes to improve the fate of farmers. Initiatives from the National Council of Agricultural Chambers have often been supported by members of the Parliament's Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, regardless of political sympathy. In addition to diligent and hard work to improve legislation in our national backyard, agricultural chambers and other organisations represent our interests in the international arena, among others. at the COGECA General Committee of Agricultural Cooperatives and the association of young farmers in the European Council of Young Farmers. The proposed bill provides for the funding from the budget of the costs associated with the participation of agricultural chambers, trade unions of individual farmers and socio-professional farmers' organisations in transnational agricultural organisations representing the professional interests of individual farmers vis-à-vis the European Union institutions; associations of young farmers in the European Council of Young Farmers (CEJA); employers' organisations in the Employers' Group of Agricultural Organisations of the European Union. This funding is planned for the period 2021 to 2026 from the State budget in the form of a targeted grant. The funding represents at least 95% of the cost of membership contributions and service costs in the Polish language of the work in these organisations. According to this draft law, funds from the co-financing are not subject to enforcement, except in cases where they have been misused, levied unduly or in excessive amounts. It is worth mentioning that the proposed law does not introduce anything new into the Polish legal system, but only prolongs the validity of the current legislation. Since 2006, the costs of participation of Polish agricultural organisations in the work of transnational agricultural organisations representing the professional interests of individual farmers towards the institutions of the European Union have been financed from the State budget. Between 2006 and 2014, aid was granted to agricultural chambers, trade unions of individual farmers and socio-professional farmers' organisations. In 2015-2020, farmers' organisations and associations representing young farmers in the European Council of Young Farmers were not the only agricultural chambers, trade unions, social and professional farmers' organisations and associations of young farmers in the European Council of Young Farmers. The draft law assumes that agricultural chambers and trade unions of individual farmers and socio-professional farmers' organisations will receive PLN 4 million annually from the state budget. Between 2015 and 2019 the grant amount was also PLN 4 million and, as the past showed, it is sufficient to cover membership contributions and to develop materials in Polish. The financing of non-governmental organisations that fight for Polish interests in the international arena and the interests of our farmers is most appropriate. The organisations that receive funding are: the National Council of Agricultural Chambers, trade unions of individual farmers, NSZZ RI, Solidarity, Trade Union of Agricultural Producers, the National Trade Union of Agricultural Farmers, Homeland, Trade Union of the National Centre of Young Farmers, socio-professional farmers' organisations, the Federation of Trade Unions of Agricultural Producers, the National Association of Sugar Burak Planters, the National Association of Producers of Ratak and Protein Plants, employers' organisations associating agricultural employers, the Federation of Employers - Dzierżawców and Agricultural Owners, associations of young farmers, the Association of Rural Youth. Mrs. Marshal! In view of the above, the Parliamentary Club Coalition Poland - Polish People's Party, Union of European Democrats, Conservatives will vote in favour of the procedated bill. It should also be noted that the equipment has not been delivered and therefore there are no costs incurred by the Treasury. As a result of this situation, an urgent government project was introduced into the Sejm, which we are debating today. The project assumes that citizens will be informed that new personal evidence will be implemented 14 days before the legislation enters into force. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u451	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It is a draft law that concerns the amendment of the Act on Personal Evidence and some other laws, print No. 1372. As we read in the explanatory memorandum, the aim of the project itself is to enable the issuing of personal evidence containing a second biometric feature in the form of fingerprints from the date set out in the communication issued by the Minister responsible for Justice. informationization, in agreement with the Minister responsible for internal, published in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland and on the subject page of the Public Information Bulletin of the Minister responsible for. IT, which sets out the date of implementation of the technical solutions enabling the issuing of identity cards with fingerprints. Unfortunately, due to issues relating to state security and public security, these devices should guarantee the security of the electronic system in which fingerprints are collected and the security of the electronic system for personalising personal evidence. The explanatory memorandum also states that, on 2 July 2021, in response to a request for technical assessment of devices enabling fingerprinting of palm finger lines to be provided by the contractor selected in an open tender procedure, the Minister of Digitisation received information from the Internal Security Agency indicating a potential threat to the security of information processed using these devices. The explanatory memorandum also states that, in view of the above, urgent action was needed to eliminate the risk of the security of information processed using these devices. It should be stressed that the implementation of the new solutions will take place immediately after the end of the tendering procedure. Notwithstanding the above, it should also be noted that, in accordance with Regulation 2019/1157, the evidence issued after 2 August 2021 will be valid until 3 August 2031 at the latest. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u479	Dear Marshal! High Seym! Józef Piłsudski, our marshal, said: A nation that does not respect its past does not deserve respect for the present and has no right to the future. Therefore, if you will allow me, from the cards of Polish history. On 1 July 1569 an act of the Lublin Sejm was signed under an agreement concluded at the initiative of Zygmunt Augustus during the General Sejm in Lublin, under which the Republic of both Nations was formed, a state composed of the Kingdom of Poland and the Grand Duchy of Lithuania, which brought together the person elected in the free election of the king, the joint Sejm and the Senate. Foreign and defence policies have been unified. Separate remains: treasure, army, law firms and offices. Customs borders have been abolished, a common coin has been introduced and an administrative system has been introduced. The aim was also to unify legislation. The Lublin Union also completed the process of unification of the Polish Crown, finally included the Oświęcim and Zatorowskie Duchy, as well as Podlasie, Volyn, Podole Eastern and Kiev region and the autonomy of Royal Prussia was abolished. The system of the Republic of both Nations is referred to as a mixed monarchy: the authorities of the former king, the Senate consisting of the highest clergy and laymen, and the noble Chamber of Members elected in local sejmikas. According to the assumptions, the authority belonged to the general nobility, which could influence the country's policy through participation in seymics and seyms. Until the end of the existence of the Republic of Poland, both Krakow nations have preserved many features of capitality, exclusive to coronation and burial of monarchs. The role of Warsaw grew since the 16th century, when it became the main meeting place of the general sejm in addition to the coronation sejm which took place in Krakow. Free elections were also held in Warsaw since 1573 and in 1596 and 1611 the monarch's court and the seat of the highest offices were moved here. At the voivodship level there were land seyms in which she had the right to participate all the nobles of the region. These sejmies elected Members to the Sejm of the Sejm. The king's position in the Republic was not strong, which was well expressed by Chancellor Jan Zamojski: the king reigns but does not rule. Each newly elected monarch was obliged to sign Henryk's articles confirming the inviolability of the rules of the state system and ensuring religious tolerance. During this period, an unusual system developed throughout Europe. While in other countries there was a centralization of power and an increase in the position of monarchs aimed at absolutism, in the Republic of Poland the power was decentralized and the nobles dominated the king in terms of competence. The Republic of both nations survived until 1795, until the partition treaties. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u483	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! In a few days, July 12th, we will celebrate the Day of Fighting and Martyrdom of the Polish Village. During World War II, the Polish village played a difficult role to overestimate. Already in September 1939, its inhabitants gave war assistance to refugees and soldiers of the Polish army. The village was based on the first guerrilla group of the Polish Army Division of Major Henryk Dobrzański, Hubala. Despite threatening repression and persecution, the Polish village showed a patriotic attitude throughout the war. Its inhabitants were part of many conspiracy groups. The village fed inhabitants of cities and guerrilla troops, sheltered Poles displaced from their homes by the occupier, kept hidden Jews. She paid a huge price for this patriotic attitude. 817 Polish villages were spaced, their inhabitants were shot, transported to concentration camps and forced labors to the Third Reich, and their possessions were robbed and destroyed, burning farms and entire villages. The symbol of the struggle and martyrdom of the inhabitants of the Polish village is Michniów in Świętokrzyskie voivodeship, who was burned with the help of the nearby guerrillas, and its inhabitants were brutally murdered on 12 July 1943, mostly by burning alive in barns. The youngest victim of pacification Stefanek Dąbrowa had only 8 days. In order to save from oblivion and commemorate the merits of the Polish village in the fight against the invader, its patriotic attitude and cruel suffering during World War II, the date of these events, 12 July, was considered a symbolic day of memory, the Day of Fighting and Martyrdom of the Polish Village. We remember. Praise the heroes! [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u493	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Municipalities across Poland are struggling with a very serious problem concerning claims of non-public kindergartens. The situation is extremely difficult and concerns hundreds of Polish local governments. For several years, municipalities across the country have been sued by non-public kindergartens to compensate them for the too low amount of the grant paid in 2009 and 2016. It is estimated that local authorities were sued by private institutions for almost PLN 17 billion. The reasons for the claim are different, as are the amounts that vary between 2% and 3% in terms of the difference between the grant due and paid, and sometimes even more than 50%. The provisions provide that all non-public kindergartens receive grants from the municipality's budget of 75% the basic amount of the grant for public kindergartens, except for the disabled pupil, not less than the amount foreseen for the disabled school pupil in the educational part of the general grant for the municipality. The owners of non-public kindergartens after years are seeking a subsidy to the wrongly calculated grants. Their actions will follow the judgment of the Supreme Court of July Ub. a year which considers that the amount requested is wrongly called a grant, and since it is not a grant, it means that it is not entitled to a child, it does not have to be spent in a given financial year and does not even have to be spent on education. It can therefore be spent on anything, even on private needs of the person who, after winning the court case, receives these funds. In that judgment, the Supreme Court held that the claim was a cash benefit and that it was not possible to waive the liability of the municipality. Thus, according to the court, this is no longer a subsidy and a civil-law relationship corresponding to the characteristics of the undertaking. The Supreme Court expressed the view that the entity entitled to receive the grant expected public funding to be involved in its activities and that its legitimate expectations would be met. The case-law so far threatens many local governments to go bankrupt. The city of Kętrzyn received actions for the amount of PLN 23 million, the city of Kwidzyń - for PLN 21 million, and in Przasnysz, non-public institutions claim the right to the amount of PLN 7 million. High Izbo! On behalf of local government officials from all over Poland, I would like to call for changes in the law that will allow to settle paid subsidies and effectively control non-public kindergartens, as well as enforcement of funds which, in accordance with the decision of the donor bodies, have been collected in excess or have been wrongly spent. Today, local colleges of appeal repeal the decisions of the granting authorities and drag them out in the course of the proceedings, even leading to their limitation. In addition, I would like to point out that the measures in question for non-public kindergartens and paid on the basis of the Supreme Court judgment of 9 July 2020 will not be linked to the objective for which they are legally intended. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-07-sejm-34-1.u502	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! In the coming week, July 11th, we will celebrate the 78th anniversary of the Volyn crime. In 1943, the Ukrainian Uprising Army, together with the Ukrainian civilian population, carried out a coordinated attack on the Polish inhabitants of 150 villages in Wołyń. It was the culmination of the wave of cruel murder and expulsion of Poles from their homes which had resulted in the brutally killed or expelled hundreds of thousands of Poles since the beginning of 1943. According to the resolution of the Polish Sejm of 22 July 2016, 11 July was established the National Day of Remembrance of Victims of the Genocide of the residents of the Second Polish Republic by Ukrainian nationalists. The historical truth has won. The times of war are always filled with cruelty, fear and death. 78 years ago, however, there was a crime involving a neighbor or acquaintance of the victim. This crime has marked the whole Polish nation and our history. July 11 is the day on which the actions against the Polish people of Volyn culminated. The fraction of Stepan Bandera in the Ukrainian Nationalist Organization and the subjugated UPA together with the Ukrainian civilian population have carried out mass extermination of our compatriots living in the area. UPA's plans were to cover as many towns as possible with extermination in order to surprise Poles and prevent them from defending themselves. The action was coordinated, preceded by a concentration of UPA troops in different locations to make their attack as effective as possible. The cruelty of this action is also reflected in the day of beginning, on which the Sunday was chosen to strike Poles in churches. During the genocide carried out by the CNS and UPA in the Eastern Ends of the Second Republic more than 100,000 people died. Poles. I am proud of the fact that more and more Poles know about those events and commemorate them with dignity. I pay my respects and appreciation to the participants of these tragic events, living witnesses, who give to Poles, our young people a witness to those tragic events. And here I would particularly like to thank the Kresowian environments and all those who care that this terrible tragedy is not forgotten in our society. At the same time, it is worth reminding us to build a dialogue today, the foundation of which will be the truth. Let us appreciate the times in which we are living and what is Poland's position today in order to take full care of citizens' safety. Let this tragic anniversary be a warning of what extreme nationalism can lead to and the consequences of accepting hate speech toward another human being.
